These plastics are divided into smaller particles with physical and chemical effects. These parts are called microplastics, which are less than 5 millimeters in size. Microplastics are probably the most dangerous class of plastic pollutants around. Microplastics are micro-nano pollutants caused by disintegration, decay. These microscopic particles can have negative effects on life. Some examples of microplastics can be listed as particulates formed by production rashes, microbicles used in cosmetics, synthetic textile fibers, car tire rashes, and chemical factors from larger plastic parts. Microplastics can reach humans by building and moving through the food pyramid through vertebrate creatures such as the invertebrates and fish that filter out water in environments such as the stream, the lake, or the sea. They also create a threatening pollution of many living creatures, including humans, because of the transition of chemicals to the water system. Congressmans, a single synthetic suit can be washed into 1,900 germistic fiber sewers. A single skin-cleaning product can contain 360,000 microbones. After use, these particles are transported to the environment as pollutants. In wastewater treatment facilities, these microbones are not kept properly, but are transferred to sensory environments along with purified water. When it reaches natural water sources, it poses a major threat to microscopic and large creatures. Microplastics, such as heavy metals, include additives, carrying toxic pollutants on their surfaces, being swallowed up by living beings, being completely irrefutable in wastewater treatment facilities, being easily transported in the atmosphere and water sources, and being very difficult to destroy in nature. Microplastics are causing the contaminants they carry to flow into the food chain and into the animal found at the top. After taking plastic parts into their bodies, various organisms have serious problems with their digestive, ejaculation, reproduction and growth systems. Microplastics are found to be found in foods offered to human consumption; in water products such as salt, clams, fish; in sugar, honey, soda, water, food and beverages; in domestic, outside air samples. Therefore, it is clear that we can be exposed to germs through diet and left, but the effects on human health are not yet fully known. In the event of people breathing or swallowing, germs can build up in our bodies and affect the immune system and show particle poisoning. In terms of the use and sustainability of water resources found in our country, germs, which are an important pollution of water and air quality, need to be handled and examined with great seriousness. Dear lawmakers, I would like to point out that as I end my words, the plastic pollution in the stream and basins is the root source of pollution in the sea, which is a very serious problem given the source of fresh water. tbmm-2020-07-22sit03spe0152par0059	Thank you.
tbmm-2020-07-22sit03spe0153par0060	Mr. President, I'm bringing to the agenda of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey a problem that will ease the economy and the real sector in the event of step-stepping, dissolution, and concerns the taxpayer of hundreds of thousands of times the value tax. The problem is that the value value tax has been applied in Turkey since 1985, so it has a history of about thirty-six years, and is applied in 166 countries around the world. It's a very modern tax, it's a tax that taxes consumption, and it's a tax on the added value created at every stage of consumption, and at the final stage, the consumer pays it. The problem here is, as you all know, the taxpayers pay a added value tax during their purchases of goods and services, and they calculate and collect a added value tax in the sale of goods and services. If the VAT, which the taxpayers paid during their purchases, is more than the VAT they calculated during their sales, they'll transfer the difference to the next period, to the next month, until they're eliminated. According to the economy's conjunctive process, it can take a very long time; it can take months, years. In companies with a particularly low investment stage or low stock-turn rate, in businesses that have taken a very long period of production, or in businesses that sell under the cost of such a crisis, for example, this added value tax is not possible, and it remains a burden on businesses. What's the truth? In fact, it's the right thing to do, to return the value-appropriate tax from the next period, to a certain amount of tax, in particular. The European Union has no directive that connects all countries in this regard. However, in the 2016/112/EC directive, which was released in 2016, the Council of Europe says: "If the VAT, which was paid during the purchases, is more than the VAT, then member states can return it or transfer it to the next period. There are many countries who have left a right to acknowledge it, but if we look at it, there are many countries who are doing it to return it. I will not waste your time counting all of these countries: Britain, Spain, Portugal, Sweden, Germany, France, Ireland, Greece, Denmark, Austria, Malta, the Netherlands, Belgium -- including the Czech Republic, South Cyprus, Croatia -- the last countries to enter the EU -- all of which extradite this VAT. In fact, some countries do it just the following month. For example, Britain is returning this month's -- this month's -- in June -- to the taxpayers immediately in July the unsustainable VAT. Some countries are going further, creating different categories to prevent the existing VAT. Turkey has to apply it, get into this system. At the moment, this is exactly 191 billion Turkish liras, according to a report issued by the Tax Council in 2019, that the VAT taxpayers will receive from the state. In January 2018, when Mr. Naci Ağbal was Minister of Finance, a bill came to the Grand National Assembly of Turkey, and there was this clause in the Plan and Budget Commission, which was later released during the General Assembly talks. I, along with my friends of the Republican People's Party, proposed a law on this matter four years ago, Turkey is waiting in the Grand National Assembly, repeating it every new era. I have brought an arrangement for the return of this tax to the Grand National Assembly of Turkey under certain conditions, but so far it has not been raised.

tbmm-2020-07-22sit03spe0176par0190	Mr. President, I have taken a pledge on behalf of the National Movement Party Group on behalf of the entire Law Proposal 225, which has been discussed, and I hereby salute you all. Under the first article of the law proposal, the Commission of Digital Mechras is established within the Turkish Grand National Assembly, where the mandates and powers of this commission are organised. With science and technology moving rapidly, the Internet today has become widely used. There have been developments in the field of smartphones and social media communication that have changed life radically. However, these developments, which quickly provide information and facilitate life, have significant benefits, as well as negatives and threats to humanity. Social media has turned into a dark well, a platform with no mercy and no remorse. Social media has become the scene of slander, betrayal, and the arena. The existence of social media, which provokes human dignity, human dignity, human aggression, in this way, is completely counterintuitive to the mind, morality, and human values. Many countries, especially Britain, Germany, France, have made legal arrangements to remove the content of the social media platforms that blame the removal of content that violate their own laws, in the case of violent, terrorist-related, cyber-bullying and child abuse, and in the event that necessary steps are not taken. In our country, there is an urgent need for a comprehensive legal arrangement on social media and is already late. In this regard, the bill offered to Parliament on 30 April 2020 by Mr. Halil Ozturk, our MP for the National Movement Movement Party, provides social network providers with certain obligations, with heavy administrative fines laid down in order to prevent the opening of a false account. As of yesterday, it was submitted to Parliamentary Chair of the Law on the Editing of Broadcasts in the Internet Environment, signed by the EC PARTY and Nationalist Action Party MPs, and to change the Law on Fighting Crimes Through These Broadcasts. The proposal organises such issues as increasing administrative fines for lawmakers who fail to meet their legal obligations, the decision to remove content instead of violating access to personal rights, the determination to appoint at least one person in Turkey's social network providers, whose daily access is more than 1 million, as representatives in Turkey and those who do not comply with it. Hopefully, as soon as the law is passed by law, the measures for the harmful aspects of social media will be taken. Their claim that this arrangement will eliminate freedom of expression is never true. Violation of personality rights, slander, defamation, blasphemy, and harassment cannot be freedom of expression; attacking human dignity, national and spiritual values cannot be considered freedom of speech; child abuse, cyberbullying, and immorality cannot be freedom of expression. Given the obligations of states to protect basic rights and freedoms, the well-being of society, public order and the establishment of national security, there is no doubt that under the authority of sovereignty, there are legal laws to establish and apply them. The proposed arrangement for social media sets new rules for this, as well as for all our citizens, especially our children and our young ones, to protect our human, national and spiritual values from crimes committed via the Internet. This issue is political, so all of our MPs must support and contribute to this law proposal. Dear lawmakers are taking measures to support the economy that has been negatively affected, while all countries have taken great pains in health care in the face of the worldwide pandemic of coronavirus. Turkey has taken historical and effective measures against the epidemic in every area. In order to reduce the negative impact of the epidemic on our economy, the necessary measures to support our citizens affected by this process have been quickly put into action. This law proposal includes a number of arrangements for the labour market, particularly in connection with earlier measures, in order to ensure that economic and social life return to normalisation, as well as the negative effects of the epidemic. With Law number 7226 released in March this year, conditions for short work grants for short study applications have been facilitated, and the President has been authorized to extend his application until December 31, 2020. The arrangement with Article 3 of this proposal allows for a separate or whole extension of the application period and the short working grant, considering the different impact of the epidemic among the sectors. The fourth article of the proposal provides a very important incentive to return to normal work at workplaces. According to this, three months from the Unemployment Insurance Fund are organised to ensure that short-term workers and paid vacationers return to their weekly normal working hours at their workplace and that all of their employer's shares will not go past 31 December 2020. The president is authorized to extend the three-month period sectorally, separately or as a whole, to up to six months. This will allow workers who have been granted a free leave of absence of 1.752 euros a month, and workers who receive 1.177 lira per month, to receive at least $2.324 per month. It is described as employer's support, but it is clear that our workers will receive normal wages instead of short work grants, which are very inadequate, or cash allowances, and therefore an application that supports our workers. Law number 7244, issued in April of this year, has been determined not to be annulled by the employer, except for three months in violation of the rules of morality and goodwill of workers. The arrangement with Article 5 of the proposal includes the end of the deadline in fixed-term employment or service contracts, the closure of the workplace for any reason and the termination of its activity, the end of any service purchases according to its relevant legislation and the end of the work in the process of termination. In addition, the President's mandate to extend for up to six months is extended to June 30, 2021, with three months each. The law proposal also provides provision for the effect of the coronavirus process, which includes extensions on some issues. This framework envisions an extended period of time on July 7th, 2020, given to the owners of public transportation to complete the shortcomings as part of the proposal being accessible to the disabled. Under Article 9 of the proposal, the amendment of the decree adopted on the appointment of occupational safety specialists and workplace physicians at workplaces with public workplaces and less than 50° staff and less dangerous classrooms is scheduled to continue until 31 December 2023. Essentially, these issues, which have been extended several times before, should be questioned as to why they have not been carried out so far, and measures should be taken. In addition, instead of extending the sentence on running a business safety expert who appears to be incapable of implementing it for another three and a half years, it would be appropriate to arrange a radical solution to this issue, such as occupational security, and to implement it immediately. However, it is also necessary to ensure that occupational safety experts be restored to a more stable legal status, to address their unemployment concerns, to resolve their problems with wages and working times. The law proposal also includes materials for our institutions and for the needs of our citizens. In this framework, the first article of the proposal is to pay the professors assigned to the Turkish Academy of Sciences on the basis of the application in TÜBITAK. The 6th article of the proposal envisions the payment of severance compensations for workers working in the Atabacas and Geventepe mines, which are part of the Redevance contracts in Manisa Soma. It is a pleasure that workers who work in some mines in Soma are unable to receive severance pay, which has been on the agenda for many years, is that our National Movement Party Deputy and Manisa MP, Mr. Erkan Akçay, has brought to the agenda a subject that we closely follow as a party. However, there must be a solution to the compensations of workers who work in our Soma and Ermenek districts and other redevans contracts, as well as elsewhere, to ensure that our workers can be compensated for their rights. Article 8 of the proposal is subject to the same regulations as local license plates in terms of administrative fines carried out in violations of road crossings of our citizens who come to our country with foreign license plates. In this way, a victim's sacrifice in our country is being taken care of by foreign license plates of citizens living abroad, especially our citizens, who we call European Turks. Measures continue to be taken to eliminate the negative impact of the Coronavirus epidemic on our citizens. According to a statement issued by the Trade Minister, it was said that loan debt for the trade and artists to the Credit and Bail Co-op Cooperates and to follow ahead of 30 June 2020 would be restructured, thus reducing the debt of about 220 000 tradesmen and artists to the overdue $2 billion, and that it would be possible to reduce the interest of an estimated $322m delay. Our farmer's Agricultural Loan Co-operatives and agricultural loans to the Agriculture Bank must also be structured under appropriate circumstances.
